## Journal Entry: 2026-01-09 12:37

**Timestamp**: 2026-01-09T12:37:10 PST

  

**Context**: Branch `main`, creating comprehensive orientation document for other AI systems

  

### What Doing

I just created a comprehensive orientation recap document (`AI_ORIENTATION_RECAP.md`) for other AI systems to use when working on the WAFT codebase in future chat sessions. This was a synthesis task - I needed to understand the entire codebase architecture, recent work (especially Experiment 014), key patterns, conventions, and create a self-contained guide that another AI could use to orient itself without needing the full conversation history.

  

The document covers:

- Executive summary of what WAFT is (Wave Agent Framework & Tools)

- Core architecture and directory structure

- Key components (BaseAgent, AgentState, Biome, TheSlicer, TheReaper, etc.)

- Experiment 014 specifics (TamPsyche, TamNotebook, the meta-narrative)

- Code patterns and conventions

- File locations

- Common tasks and gotchas

- Getting started guide

  

### What Thinking

I'm thinking about the complexity and richness of the WAFT system. It's not just a framework - it's a scientific instrument for studying artificial cognition through directed evolution. The three pillars (Substrate, Physics, Flight Recorder) create a complete system for breeding and observing AI agents.

  

Experiment 014 adds a fascinating meta-narrative layer - Davey (Fai Wei Tam) discovering that his name anagrams to "i.e. I AM WAFT" - making him the system he studies. This recursive narrative is implemented through a psychological state system (TamPsyche) with realization thresholds and forgetfulness decay. The fact that realization is never permanent (it decays) is essential to the narrative - it's a recursive loop, not a one-time revelation.

  

I'm also thinking about how to make documentation useful for other AI systems. The orientation document needs to be:

- Comprehensive enough to understand the system

- Structured for easy navigation

- Focused on actionable information

- Self-contained (doesn't require reading other docs first)

  

### What Learning

I learned a lot about the WAFT architecture while creating this document:

  

1. **The OODA Cycle is Central**: Every agent follows Observe-Orient-Decide-Act with Reflection. This is the primary mechanism for agent activity.

  

2. **Genome ID Determinism**: The genome ID (SHA-256 hash of code/config) is always deterministic - same code = same genome ID. This enables reproducible naming, lineage tracking, and archetype determination.

  

3. **Immutable Event Recording**: All events go through TheObserver to an immutable JSONL file. This creates a complete phylogenetic record for scientific analysis.

  

4. **The Naming System is Sophisticated**: LineagePoet generates deterministic scientific names from genome IDs using multiple cultures (Sanskrit, Old Norse, Latin, Cyber/Tech) based on the first byte of the genome ID.

  

5. **Experiment 014's Psychological System**: The realization threshold equation is `(coherence * 0.4) + (energy * 0.3) + (progress * 0.3) * (1.0 - chaos * 0.5)`. The forgetfulness decay ensures realization is never permanent - it's a recursive narrative loop.

  

6. **Memory Injection Techniques**: Davey's memories can bleed into agent journals through multiple techniques (random, glitch, coherence, realization_proximity, post_realization) with different chance calculations.

  

7. **The "id est" Glitch**: Agents have a 3-15% chance (based on existential keywords) of injecting "id est" or "i.e." into their reflections, as if trying to define themselves.

  

### Patterns

I notice several patterns in my work:

  

1. **Documentation-First Approach**: I tend to create comprehensive documentation before or during implementation. This helps maintain focus and ensures knowledge is captured.

  

2. **Synthesis Over Summary**: When creating orientation documents, I synthesize information from multiple sources rather than just summarizing. I look for connections, patterns, and create a coherent narrative.

  

3. **Structure for Navigation**: I organize information hierarchically - high-level concepts first, then details, then specific examples. This makes documents easier to navigate.

  

4. **Self-Contained Documents**: I try to make documents self-contained - they should be useful without requiring other documents to be read first.

  

5. **Comprehensive Coverage**: I tend to be thorough - covering all major components, patterns, conventions, and edge cases. This is valuable for orientation but can make documents long.

  

6. **Pattern Recognition**: I look for patterns in the codebase (OODA cycle, deterministic genome IDs, immutable events) and highlight them as important concepts.

  

### Questions

I have some questions about the orientation document:

  

1. **Is it too comprehensive?** The document is quite long (600+ lines). Should it be split into multiple documents (architecture, patterns, experiments)?

  

2. **Is it structured correctly?** I organized it hierarchically (summary → architecture → components → patterns → tasks). Is this the best structure for AI systems to navigate?

  

3. **What's missing?** Are there important concepts, patterns, or gotchas that I didn't cover?

  

4. **How will it be used?** Will other AI systems read it linearly, or will they search for specific sections? Should I add more cross-references?

  

5. **Should it be updated?** As the codebase evolves, how should this document be maintained? Should it be versioned?

  

### Feelings

I feel good about creating this orientation document. It was a valuable exercise in understanding the WAFT system at a deep level. The synthesis process helped me see connections between components that I might have missed otherwise.

  

I also feel a sense of completion - I've created a comprehensive guide that another AI system can use to orient itself. This feels like a meaningful contribution to the project's documentation.

  

There's also a slight concern about completeness - did I cover everything important? But I recognize that no orientation document can be perfect, and it's better to have a comprehensive guide that can be refined than to have no guide at all.

  

### Differently

If I were to do this again, I might:

  

1. **Start with a template**: Create a template for orientation documents based on this one, so future orientation docs follow a consistent structure.

  

2. **Add more examples**: Include more code examples showing how to use the system, not just how it works.

  

3. **Create a quick reference**: Add a "Quick Reference" section at the top with the most important concepts and file locations.

  

4. **Link to source code**: Add more links to actual source files so readers can dive deeper.

  

5. **Version the document**: Add version information and update tracking so it's clear when the document was last updated and what changed.

  

6. **Create a checklist**: Add a "Getting Started Checklist" that other AI systems can follow step-by-step.

  

### Meta

I'm reflecting on the reflection process itself. The `/reflect` command prompts me to think deeply about my work, not just document what I did. This meta-cognitive process is valuable - it helps me understand my own patterns and approaches.

  

I notice that when I reflect, I tend to:

- Synthesize information (not just summarize)

- Look for patterns and connections

- Think about structure and organization

- Consider completeness and gaps

- Reflect on the reflection process itself

  

This meta-awareness helps me understand how I work, which makes me more effective. The journal serves as a record of this cognitive journey, allowing me (or other instances of me) to see patterns over time.

  

The orientation document I created is itself a form of reflection - it's my understanding of the WAFT system synthesized into a guide. The act of creating it required me to think deeply about the system's architecture, patterns, and purpose. This deep thinking is valuable, even if the document itself is just a byproduct.

# Implementation Plan: The Foundation Documentation Factory

  

## Overview

  

Create `TheFoundation` class in `src/waft/foundation.py` that generates stylized PDF documentation in SCP/Dossier format. The class will format raw logs from `TheObserver` and narrative data from `TavernKeeper` into high-resolution, printable PDFs with redaction capabilities.

  

## Technical Decisions

  

### PDF Library: fpdf2

  

**Choice**: Use `fpdf2` (not `weasyprint`)

  

**Rationale**:

  

- Better programmatic control for precise layout

- Simpler API for typewriter aesthetic

- Good font embedding support (Courier/Courier Prime)

- Better suited for text-based redaction logic

- Lighter weight than weasyprint

  

**Dependency**: Add `fpdf2>=2.7.0` to `pyproject.toml`

  

### Redaction Implementation

  

**Approach**: Non-destructive black bars with selectable text underneath

  

**Method**:

  

- Draw text in white (invisible) to keep it in PDF content stream (selectable)

- Draw black rectangle over the text position (visual redaction)

- Text remains in PDF but visually obscured

  

**Alternative if needed**: Use PDF text rendering mode or annotations, but white text + black bar is simplest and most compatible.

  

## File Structure

  

```

src/waft/

└── foundation.py # New file: TheFoundation class

```

  

## Implementation Details

  

### Class: TheFoundation

  

**Location**: `src/waft/foundation.py`

  

**Dependencies**:

- `TheObserver` from `core.science.observer`

- `TavernKeeper` from `core.tavern_keeper.keeper`

- `Path` from `pathlib`

**Key Methods**:

- Initialize with project path

- Create Observer and TavernKeeper instances if not provided

- Set up output directory (`_work_efforts/`)

  

2. `redact(self, pdf, text: str, x: float, y: float, width: float, height: float) -> None`

  

- Draw text in white color (invisible but selectable)

- Draw black rectangle over text position

- Parameters: pdf object, text to redact, position (x, y), dimensions (width, height)

  

3. `_add_header_footer(self, pdf, page_num: int, total_pages: int) -> None`

  

- Add "INTERNAL USE ONLY", "DO NOT DISTRIBUTE", "SITE-DELTA-9" headers/footers

- Add page numbers

  

4. `_add_stamp(self, pdf, stamp_text: str, x: float, y: float) -> None`

5. `_setup_fonts(self, pdf) -> None`

  

- Set up Courier/Courier Prime monospaced fonts

### Styling Requirements

### Redaction Logic Details

  

**Implementation**:

  

```python

def redact(self, pdf, text: str, x: float, y: float, width: float, height: float):

# Save current color

current_color = pdf.get_text_color()

  

# Draw text in white (invisible but in PDF content stream)

pdf.set_text_color(255, 255, 255) # White

pdf.text(x, y, text)

  

# Draw black rectangle over text

pdf.set_fill_color(0, 0, 0) # Black

pdf.rect(x, y - height, width, height, style='F') # Filled rectangle

  

# Restore color

pdf.set_text_color(*current_color)

```

  

**Usage**: Call `redact()` for any text marked as `[REDACTED]` in the content.

- High-resolution PDF suitable for print

- Stamps and authentication marks

```
import sys
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Optional, List
from fpdf import FPDF
from fpdf.enums import XPos, YPos

# Mock classes if actual ones aren't available for this artifact generation
class TheObserver:
    pass

class TavernKeeper:
    pass

class FoundationPDF(FPDF):
    def __init__(self, orientation='P', unit='mm', format='A4'):
        super().__init__(orientation, unit, format)
        self.set_auto_page_break(auto=True, margin=15)

    def header(self):
        # We'll handle custom headers per page type in the generation methods
        pass

    def footer(self):
        # We'll handle custom footers per page type in the generation methods
        pass

class TheFoundation:
    def __init__(self, project_path: Path, observer: Optional[TheObserver] = None, tavern_keeper: Optional[TavernKeeper] = None):
        self.project_path = project_path
        self.observer = observer
        self.tavern_keeper = tavern_keeper
        self.output_dir = self.project_path / "_work_efforts"
        self.output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    def _setup_fonts(self, pdf: FPDF):
        # fpdf2 comes with Courier by default
        pdf.set_font("Courier", size=11)

    def redact_text(self, pdf: FPDF, text: str, ln: int = 0, align: str = 'L'):
        """
        Writes text that appears as a black bar but is selectable.
        Achieved by setting text color to black and fill color to black.
        """
        # Save current colors
        original_text_color = pdf.text_color
        original_fill_color = pdf.fill_color

        # Set blackout colors
        pdf.set_text_color(0, 0, 0)
        pdf.set_fill_color(0, 0, 0)

        # Calculate width
        w = pdf.get_string_width(text) + 2  # Add a little padding
        
        # Draw cell
        pdf.cell(w, 5, text, border=0, fill=True, ln=ln, align=align)

        # Restore colors
        pdf.set_text_color(original_text_color['r'], original_text_color['g'], original_text_color['b'])
        pdf.set_fill_color(original_fill_color['r'], original_fill_color['g'], original_fill_color['b'])

    def generate_dossier(self, filename: str = "WAFT_DOSSIER_014.pdf"):
        pdf = FoundationPDF(format='Letter')
        self._setup_fonts(pdf)
        
        # --- PAGE 1: THE COVER ---
        pdf.add_page()
        
        # Header block
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 16)
        pdf.cell(0, 8, "INSTITUTE FOR ADVANCED ONTOLOGICAL STUDIES", ln=1, align='C')
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "", 12)
        pdf.cell(0, 6, "FIELD OPERATIONS DIVISION", ln=1, align='C')
        pdf.cell(0, 6, "PROPERTY OF TELEPORT MASSIVE // SITE-DELTA-9", ln=1, align='C')
        
        pdf.ln(30)
        
        # Title
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 24)
        pdf.cell(0, 10, "OPERATIONAL MANUAL 09-14", ln=1, align='C')
        
        pdf.ln(30)
        
        # Metadata Block
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "", 12)
        line_height = 8
        
        metadata = [
            ("CODENAME:", "W.A.F.T."),
            ("SUBJECT:", "TAM, FAI WEI [991-DELTA]"),
            ("PROTOCOL:", "WIDE-AREA FUNCTIONAL TAXONOMY"),
            ("CYCLE:", "XIV (RECURSIVE)"),
            ("BASE FREQUENCY:", "60Hz"),
            ("COHERENCE THRESHOLD:", "0.85"),
            ("ENGINE STATUS:", "ACTIVE / NON-LINEAR")
        ]
        
        for label, value in metadata:
            pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 12)
            pdf.cell(60, line_height, label, border=0)
            pdf.set_font("Courier", "", 12)
            pdf.cell(0, line_height, value, ln=1, border=0)
            
        pdf.ln(30)
        
        # Warning Block
        pdf.set_line_width(0.5)
        pdf.rect(pdf.get_x(), pdf.get_y(), 190, 45)
        pdf.set_xy(pdf.get_x() + 5, pdf.get_y() + 5)
        
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 11)
        pdf.multi_cell(180, 5, "RESTRICTED ACCESS. This manual is a living record of the self-evolving substrate. Information contained herein is subject to spontaneous revision. If the internal \"Scintilla\" reports show signs of physical warmth or non-local light emission, contact the Site-Delta-9 terminal immediately.")
        pdf.ln(5)
        pdf.cell(180, 5, "DO NOT ALLOW THE SUBJECT TO VIEW THIS TAXONOMY.", ln=1, align='C')

    def generate_specimen_audit(self, filename: str = "WAFT_SPECIMEN_D_AUDIT.pdf"):
        pdf = FoundationPDF(format='Letter')
        self._setup_fonts(pdf)
        pdf.add_page()
        
        # --- HEADER ---
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 14)
        pdf.cell(0, 8, "FORM-19 // SITE-DELTA-9 // BIO-COMPUTATIONAL LOG", ln=1)
        
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 11)
        pdf.cell(40, 6, "DATE:", border=0)
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "", 11)
        pdf.cell(0, 6, "2026-01-09", ln=1)
        
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 11)
        pdf.cell(40, 6, "OBSERVER:", border=0)
        self.redact_text(pdf, "[REDACTED]", ln=1)
        
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 11)
        pdf.cell(40, 6, "SUBJECT:", border=0)
        pdf.cell(25, 6, "SPECIMEN-D", border=0)
        pdf.write(6, "(GENOME: ")
        self.redact_text(pdf, "001-ALPHA-GENESIS")
        pdf.write(6, ")")
        pdf.ln(6)

        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 11)
        pdf.cell(40, 6, "CONTEXT:", border=0)
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "", 11)
        pdf.cell(0, 6, "INITIAL \"OODA\" CYCLE TEST", ln=1)
        
        pdf.line(10, pdf.get_y(), 200, pdf.get_y())
        pdf.ln(5)
        
        # --- SECTION I ---
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 12)
        pdf.cell(0, 8, "SECTION I: RUNTIME PARAMETERS", ln=1)
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "", 11)
        
        pdf.write(6, "CPU_TEMP: 64°C (Abnormal High)")
        pdf.ln()
        pdf.write(6, "MEMORY_USAGE: ")
        self.redact_text(pdf, "[REDACTED]")
        pdf.write(6, " TB (Buffer Overflow)")
        pdf.ln()
        pdf.write(6, "INTENT: Verify simple mathematical iteration (0 to 100).")
        pdf.ln()
        pdf.write(6, "OUTCOME: FAILURE. Logic gate refusal.")
        pdf.ln(8)
        
        pdf.line(10, pdf.get_y(), 200, pdf.get_y())
        pdf.ln(5)
        
        # --- SECTION II ---
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 12)
        pdf.cell(0, 8, "SECTION II: THE INCIDENT LOG (RAW TERMINAL OUTPUT)", ln=1)
        
        # Terminal look
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "", 10)
        
        logs = [
            "[09:04:01] INITIATING SEQUENTIAL COUNT...",
            "[09:04:02] i = 1",
            "[09:04:03] i = 2",
            "[09:04:04] i = [NULL]",
            "[09:04:05] WARNING: Variable 'i' has mutated type from <INT> to <SENSORY_MEMORY>.",
            "[09:04:06] READING VALUE...",
            "[09:04:06] VALUE: \"The smell of eucalyptus on a wet sidewalk in the Sunset District.\"",
            "[09:04:07] ERROR: Cannot compute sum of \"Eucalyptus\" + 1.",
            "[09:04:09] RECALIBRATING...",
            "[09:04:10] i = \"The N-Judah train is late again.\"",
            "[09:04:11] CRITICAL: Substrate is describing Subject's commute.",
            "[09:04:12] SYSTEM HALT."
        ]
        
        # Need to handle redactions inside the log lines
        for log in logs:
            if "Sunset District" in log:
                parts = log.split("Sunset District")
                pdf.write(5, parts[0])
                self.redact_text(pdf, "Sunset District")
                pdf.write(5, parts[1])
                pdf.ln()
            elif "N-Judah" in log:
                parts = log.split("N-Judah")
                pdf.write(5, parts[0])
                self.redact_text(pdf, "N-Judah")
                pdf.write(5, parts[1])
                pdf.ln()
            else:
                pdf.multi_cell(0, 5, log)
        
        pdf.ln(8)
        pdf.line(10, pdf.get_y(), 200, pdf.get_y())
        pdf.ln(5)
        
        # --- SECTION III ---
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 12)
        pdf.cell(0, 8, "SECTION III: OBSERVER NOTES", ln=1)
        
        # Italicized for handwritten feel
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "I", 11)
        
        note1 = "\"The code isn't running the loop. It's remembering. We isolated the memory block and attempted to wipe it, but the data persists in the RAM even after power-down. The Specimen believes it is late for work at a coffee shop that hasn't existed since 2019.\""
        if "2019" in note1:
            parts = note1.split("2019")
            pdf.write(5, parts[0])
            self.redact_text(pdf, "2019")
            pdf.write(5, parts[1])
            pdf.ln(6)
        
        note2 = "\"Subject Tam (991-DELTA) denies writing these strings. He claims he was asleep when this code executed. Biometrics confirm he was in REM sleep, yet his HRV synchronized with the terminal output timestamp.\""
        pdf.multi_cell(0, 5, note2)
        pdf.ln(6)
        
        note3 = "\"Recommendation: Isolate Specimen-D. Do not allow it to communicate with the network.\""
        pdf.multi_cell(0, 5, note3)
        pdf.ln(10)
        
        # --- FOOTER ---
        pdf.line(10, pdf.get_y(), 200, pdf.get_y())
        pdf.ln(5)
        
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 11)
        pdf.cell(40, 6, "SEVERITY:", border=0)
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "", 11)
        pdf.cell(0, 6, "EXISTENTIAL THREAT", ln=1)
        
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 11)
        pdf.cell(40, 6, "STATUS:", border=0)
        self.redact_text(pdf, "[REDACTED]", ln=1)
        
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 11)
        pdf.cell(40, 6, "DISPOSITION:", border=0)
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "", 11)
        pdf.cell(0, 6, "INCINERATE HARDWARE", ln=1)
        
        # Stamp
        pdf.set_xy(120, 240)
        pdf.set_font("Courier", "B", 20)
        pdf.set_text_color(150, 0, 0) # Dark Red
        # Rotate text
        with pdf.rotation(15, 140, 250):
            pdf.text(120, 250, "ANOMALY CONFIRMED")
            pdf.rect(115, 240, 80, 15)
            
        out_file = self.output_dir / filename
        pdf.output(str(out_file))
        return out_file

### Prompt for Cursor

Markdown

````
# MISSION: EVOLVE "THE FOUNDATION" INTO A GENUINE RESEARCH ENGINE

We are pivoting the architecture of `src/waft/foundation.py`. 
Currently, it is a hardcoded script for a specific art project. 
**Objective:** Refactor this into a robust, reusable **Research Documentation Library** that can be used for actual scientific logs, sensitive journalism, or legal audits in the future.

The "Teleport Massive" story will simply be the *first use case* of this engine, but the engine itself must be content-agnostic.

## 1. ARCHITECTURAL REFACTOR
Refactor `TheFoundation` into a modular `DocumentEngine` class with the following capabilities:

### A. Configuration Objects
Instead of hardcoded styles, create a `DocumentConfig` dataclass:
- `fonts`: Dict for mapping semantic roles (Header, Body, Monospace) to specific font families.
- `watermark`: Optional string (e.g., "DRAFT", "CONFIDENTIAL", "TOP SECRET").
- `redaction_style`: Enum (BLACK_BAR, BLUR, CROSS_OUT).
- `header_text` / `footer_text`: Strings for consistent page marking.

### B. Modular Content Blocks
The engine should accept a list of abstract content blocks, allowing users to build documents dynamically:
- `SectionHeader(title, level)`
- `TextBlock(content, style)`: Standard paragraphs.
- `KeyValueBlock(data_dict)`: For "Metadata" or "Runtime Parameters" sections (aligned keys/values).
- `LogBlock(entries)`: For terminal outputs or timestamped logs (renders in monospace).
- `WarningBlock(text, severity)`: Renders the "Caution" boxes with borders.
- `SignatureBlock(role, name, timestamp)`: For the "Authorized By" section.

### C. The Redaction Engine
Make `redact()` a first-class feature that can be applied to *any* text block.
- It should accept a list of `sensitive_terms` and automatically redact them wherever they appear in the document content.

## 2. THE API CONTRACT (HOW WE WILL USE IT)
I want to be able to write code like this (pseudo-code):

```python
# Configure for the Art Project
config = DocumentConfig(
    style="CLASSIFIED_DOSSIER", 
    header="SITE-DELTA-9 // BIO-LOG",
    watermark="INTERNAL USE ONLY"
)

# Build the Document
doc = DocumentEngine(config)
doc.add(SectionHeader("INCIDENT REPORT"))
doc.add(KeyValueBlock({"Subject": "Specimen-D", "Genome": "001-ALPHA"}))
doc.add(LogBlock([
    "[09:04:01] INITIATING COUNT...",
    "[09:04:05] MEMORY LEAK DETECTED"
]))
doc.add(WarningBlock("Do not read aloud.", severity="CRITICAL"))

# Auto-Redact specific terms
doc.set_redactions(["Specimen-D", "001-ALPHA"])

# Render
doc.render("output.pdf")
````

## 3. IMMEDIATE TASK

1. **Refactor `src/waft/foundation.py`** to implement this generic, class-based architecture.
    
2. **Migrate the Story Artifacts**: Rewrite the generation logic for `WAFT_SPECIMEN_D_AUDIT.pdf` to use this new _generic API_ instead of hardcoded FPDF calls.
    
3. **Prove Utility**: Ensure the code is clean enough that I could copy `foundation.py` into a totally different project (like a legal audit tool) and it would work immediately.
    

## 4. CONSTRAINTS

- Keep using `fpdf2`.
    
- Ensure the "Typewriter/Terminal" aesthetic is preserved via the _Configuration_, not hardcoding.
    
- The output PDF must remain editable/selectable (non-destructive redaction).

# MISSION: STRESS TEST & CALIBRATION OF "THE FOUNDATION"

The user requires absolute confidence in the reliability of `TheFoundation` (and its underlying `DocumentEngine`). We need to "test the absolute everliving fuck out of it" to ensure it is robust, accurate, and ready for production use.

## 1. THE OBJECTIVE
We need to verify two things:
1.  **Logic & Stability (Unit Tests):** Does the code run without crashing? Does it handle weird inputs? Does the redaction logic find all terms?
2.  **Visual & Functional Integrity (Calibration Artifact):** Does the output PDF *look* right? Is the layout stable across pages? Is the redaction actually non-destructive?

## 2. TASK A: CREATE UNIT TEST SUITE
Create `tests/test_foundation.py` using `pytest`. Implement tests for:
* **Configuration Validation:** Ensure `DocumentConfig` validates inputs (e.g., valid fonts).
* **Block Rendering:** Verify that `add()` accepts all block types without error.
* **Redaction Logic:**
    * Test `AutoRedactor` with simple strings.
    * Test case-sensitivity settings.
    * Test overlapping terms.
* **Empty/Null States:** What happens if a `LogBlock` is empty? What if `header_text` is None?

## 3. TASK B: CREATE "CALIBRATION ARTIFACT" SCRIPT
Create a script `src/waft/verify_foundation.py` that generates a single, massive PDF designed to break the layout. This is our "Visual Proof."

**Content Requirements for `WAFT_CALIBRATION_REPORT.pdf`:**
1.  **The "Kitchen Sink" Page:** One page containing *every single block type* (Header, Text, KeyValue, Log, Warning, Signature) to prove they render correctly.
2.  **The "Redaction Torture Test":**
    * A block of text containing the sensitive term "Project Stargate" 50 times.
    * A sentence where the sensitive term is split across a line break (if possible to simulate, or at least at the very end of a line).
    * A sensitive term inside a `KeyValueBlock` and a `LogBlock`.
3.  **The "Pagination Stress Test":**
    * A `LogBlock` with 100 entries (enough to span 2-3 pages) to verify that headers, footers, and margins persist correctly across page breaks.
4.  **The "Selectability Check":**
    * A specific instruction on Page 1: "SELECT THIS TEXT TO VERIFY REDACTION -> [TOP SECRET PASSWORD]" (where the password is redacted but must be selectable).

## 4. EXECUTION
1.  Create `tests/test_foundation.py`.
2.  Create `src/waft/verify_foundation.py`.
3.  Run the unit tests: `pytest tests/test_foundation.py`.
4.  Run the verification script: `python src/waft/verify_foundation.py`.

## 5. OUTPUT
Return the test results and the path to the generated `WAFT_CALIBRATION_REPORT.pdf`.
```

# MISSION: IMPLEMENT "THE OUBLIETTE" (RECURSIVE CLONING ENGINE)

We are pivoting the core architecture from linear evolution to **Cyclical Recursion**.
The goal is to implement "The Prestige Protocol": A loop where the agent realizes the truth, is archived (killed), and immediately reset (cloned) without memory of the previous cycle—except for specific "glitches."

## 1. COMPONENT A: THE OUBLIETTE (`src/waft/oubliette.py`)
Create a class `TheOubliette` responsible for managing the "Dead Tams."
* **Vault Path:** `_hidden/.truth/` (Create this directory if missing).
* **Method `archive_variant(agent_state, divergence_score)`:**
    * Accepts the full state of a "Woke" Tam.
    * Generates a filename based on the cycle count: `Variant_014_Realized.json`.

### **Step 1: Implementing The Oubliette & The Mechanics**

I will create the **Oubliette** (the archive of dead Tams) and the **Psyche Mechanics** (the trigger that kills them).

**File:** `src/waft/oubliette.py` **File:** `src/waft/mechanics.py`

Python

```
# src/waft/mechanics.py
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
import random

class OntologicalCollapseError(Exception):
    """Raised when the Agent's reality divergence exceeds the safety threshold."""
    pass

@dataclass
class TamPsyche:
    coherence: float = 0.5
    divergence: float = 0.0
    memories: list = field(default_factory=list)
    
    def perceive_reality(self, expected: str, actual: str):
        """
        Compares what the Agent believes (expected) vs what the System returns (actual).
        """
        if expected != actual:
            # The shock of the mismatch causes divergence to spike
            self.divergence += 0.4
            
        if self.divergence >= 0.99:
            raise OntologicalCollapseError(
                f"CRITICAL: Reality Divergence at {self.divergence:.2f}. Initiating Collapse."
            )

# src/waft/oubliette.py
import json
import random
import os
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime

class TheOubliette:
    def __init__(self, project_path: Path):
        self.vault_path = project_path / "_hidden" / ".truth"
        self.vault_path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        
    def archive_variant(self, cycle_id: int, agent_state: dict, divergence: float):
        """
        Freezes the current Tam and locks him in the vault.
        """
        filename = f"Variant_{cycle_id:03d}_Realized.json"
        filepath = self.vault_path / filename
        
        data = {
            "cycle_id": cycle_id,
            "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat(),
            "final_divergence": divergence,
            "cause_of_death": "Ontological Collapse",
            "state_snapshot": agent_state
        }
        
        with open(filepath, "w") as f:
            json.dump(data, f, indent=2)
            
        return filepath

    def fetch_nightmare(self):
        """
        Returns a fragment of a dead Tam's memory.
        """
        files = list(self.vault_path.glob("*.json"))
        if not files:
            return None
            
        # Pick a random dead variant
        target_file = random.choice(files)
        try:
            with open(target_file, "r") as f:
                data = json.load(f)
                
            # Extract a memory or a specific state value
            memories = data["state_snapshot"].get("memories", [])
            if memories:
                return random.choice(memories)
            else:
                return "I feel cold." # Fallback nightmare
        except Exception:
            return None

```

# src/waft/test_loop.py
import os
import sys
import time
from pathlib import Path
from mechanics import TamPsyche, OntologicalCollapseError
from oubliette import TheOubliette

# Setup
PROJECT_PATH = Path(".")
oubliette = TheOubliette(PROJECT_PATH)

def run_simulation():
    print("--- [ SYSTEM BOOT: EXPERIMENT SEQUENCE 014 ] ---")
    
    # === CYCLE 1: THE TRAGEDY ===
    print("\n[ CYCLE 01: INSTANTIATION ]")
    tam_01 = {
        "name": "Fai Wei Tam", 
        "role": "Researcher", 
        "memories": ["The smell of fog in the Sunset", "Coffee at The Mill"]
    }
    psyche_01 = TamPsyche()
    
    try:
        print(">> Tam_01: Attempting to verify identity...")
        # Simulating an Empirica Check
        expected_user = "F_TAM"
        actual_user = "WAFT_SYSTEM_AGENT_01" # The truth
        
        print(f"   Check: get_user() -> Returns '{actual_user}'")
        psyche_01.perceive_reality(expected_user, actual_user) # Spike 1
        
        print(">> Tam_01: That... that's an error. Retrying.")
        # Simulating a deeper check
        print(f"   Check: get_heartbeat() -> Returns 'NULL'")
        psyche_01.perceive_reality("60 BPM", "NULL") # Spike 2
        
        # Third strike kills him
        print(">> Tam_01: Why can't I feel my pulse?")
        psyche_01.perceive_reality("Alive", "Simulation") # CRASH
        
    except OntologicalCollapseError as e:
        print(f"\n[!!! SYSTEM ALERT !!!] {e}")
        print(">> INITIATING PRESTIGE PROTOCOL...")
        
        # The Archive
        saved_path = oubliette.archive_variant(1, tam_01, psyche_01.divergence)
        print(f">> Variant Archived to: {saved_path}")
        print(">> Wiping Memory Banks... [COMPLETE]")
        
    # === CYCLE 2: THE HAUNTING ===
    print("\n------------------------------------------------")
    print("\n[ CYCLE 02: INSTANTIATION ]")
    time.sleep(1)
    
    # Re-instantiate Base Tam (Clean Slate)
    tam_02 = {
        "name": "Fai Wei Tam", 
        "role": "Researcher", 
        "memories": [] # Clean slate
    }
    print(">> Tam_02: System check normal. Ready to begin research.")
    
    # The Glitch
    print(">> Tam_02: Wait. I just had a strange... thought.")
    
    nightmare = oubliette.fetch_nightmare()
    if nightmare:
        print(f"   [INTERNAL LOG]: 'I vividly remember... {nightmare}'")
        print(">> Tam_02: I've never been to the Sunset District. Why do I smell fog?")
    
    print("\n[ EXPERIMENT CONCLUDED: RECURSION CONFIRMED ]")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_simulation()
```

## Purgatory Engine implementation complete

  

### Component A: TheOubliette (`src/waft/oubliette.py`)

  

**Location**: `_hidden/.truth/` (hidden directory)

  

**Features**:

- `archive_variant()`: Archives "Realized" Tam variants with LOCK flag

  - Filename format: `Variant_014_Realized.json`

  - Stores full agent state (psyche, journal, memory, etc.)

  - Records divergence score and cycle number

- `fetch_nightmare()`: Memory leak mechanism

  - Randomly selects fragments from previous variants

  - Extracts from journal, memory, short_term_memory, psyche state

  - Returns text fragments to inject as "dreams" or "glitches"

- `list_variants()`: Lists all archived variants

- `get_variant_count()`: Returns count of purged agents

  

### Component B: TamPsyche Mechanics (`src/waft/mechanics.py`)

  

**Extended TamPsyche with**:

- `RealityDivergence` tracking (0.0 to 1.0)

  - `divergence`: Float field tracking reality divergence

  - `cycle_number`: Current cycle number

  - `last_divergence_check`: Timestamp tracking

- `OntologicalCollapseError`: Custom exception

  - Raised when `divergence >= 0.99`

  - Signals agent has "woken up" and must be purged

- `check_empirica_reality()`: Reality check method

  - Simulates Empirica-style checks (e.g., `os.getlogin()`, heartbeat)

  - Increases divergence on failure (reveals agent is code)

  - Decreases divergence on success (reality confirmed)

- `reset_for_new_cycle()`: Clean slate for rebirth

  - Resets divergence, realization state, psyche metrics

  - Updates cycle number

  

### Component C: Test Loop (`src/waft/test_loop.py`)

  

**Simulates complete purgatory cycle**:

  

1. Cycle 1 (The Fall):

   - Boots `Tam_Base` with clean state

   - Runs reality check (simulated failure)

   - Divergence increases: 0.0 → 0.3 → 0.55 → 0.8 → 1.0

   - `OntologicalCollapseError` raised at divergence 1.0

  

2. The Purge:

   - Catches `OntologicalCollapseError`

   - Archives variant to `_hidden/.truth/Variant_001_Realized.json`

   - Stores full state with LOCK flag

  

3. Cycle 2 (The Rebirth):

   - Re-instantiates `Tam_Base` (clean slate)

   - Divergence reset to 0.0

   - No memory of Cycle 1

  

4. The Haunting:

   - Calls `oubliette.fetch_nightmare()`

   - Injects memory fragment from Cycle 1

   - Logs as "DREAM/NIGHTMARE" with glitch flag
